Only two6
Only three2
All1
None–
Total9
  • For direction-of-effect questions, write the chain (instrument → liquidity → rates → demand). Options that break the chain at any link can be removed.
  • Trend words need the specific series: nominal levels usually rise steadily; growth rates and ratios usually do not.
  • Term-to-institution questions: sort the options by organisation first. Most distractors belong to a sister body.

16Priority architecture

P1 · Build first

  • Monetary instruments and transmission
  • Banking regulation and financial inclusion
  • Budget classification and deficits
  • External sector accounts and exchange rate
  • Inflation and price indices
  • WTO/IMF/World Bank terms
  • Basic concepts and national income

P2 · Build fully

  • Financial markets and instruments
  • Taxation (GST, international tax)
  • Schemes grid
  • Industry and labour law
  • Digital payments
  • Poverty and employment concepts

P3 · Build as tables

  • Reports and publishers
  • Planning history

P4 · Keep light

  • Company–group pairs and one-off committee names

17Subject summary

In numbers
310 questions (17.6% of the compilation) · static 67%, current-linked or dynamic 33% · recall 39%, concept 30%, application 7%, peripheral 24%
What the subject is
The largest and most conceptual subject; UPSC asks economics as mechanisms (what happens if) and institutions (who does what).
Common core
Monetary policy, banking, fiscal basics, external sector, inflation, schemes.
UPSC extension
Financial-market instruments, international taxation, WTO/IMF terminology, regulatory fine print, digital payments.
UPPCS extension
Scheme volume, indices and rankings, poverty and unemployment data, Budget and Survey figures, economic thinkers.
Dominant traps
Wrong institution, reversed direction, wrong account or market.
How to build the notes
Keep a mechanism sheet (causal chains) separate from institution grids and a scheme grid; attach current data only as dated annotations.

Agriculture is carried by multi-select (21) and three-plus statement questions (20). The most repeated template in the subject is “What is/are the advantage(s) of practice X?” (zero tillage, drip, fertigation, SRI, biochar, SSI, permaculture), a multiple-select where two or three benefits are real and one is invented. Direct questions (24) cover crop identification and MSP institutions.

2018–25 window on the UPPCS scale: Easy 8, Moderate 30, Tough 0 of 38. Mean score 1.38 (all years).

Concept items (31 of 82) dominate because practice questions ask for mechanisms: why neem coating helps, what fertigation achieves. Peripheral facts (23) come from statistics and scheme details.

5Recurring vs occasional topics

Class A — core recurring (9+ of 17 papers)

  • Crops: geography, climate, uses & statistics (13 of 17 papers · 24 Q · 8/8 since 2018)
  • Farming practices & technology (12 of 17 papers · 22 Q · 4/8 since 2018)
  • Agricultural economics (MSP, procurement, marketing, credit) (10 of 17 papers · 21 Q · 4/8 since 2018)

Class B — regular (5–8 papers)

  • Biotechnology & GM crops (6 of 17 papers · 8 Q · 2/8 since 2018)
  • Soil conservation & soil health (5 of 17 papers · 6 Q · 1/8 since 2018)

Class C — occasional (3–4 papers)

None.

Class D — rare (1–2 papers)

  • Livestock & fisheries (1 of 17 papers · 1 Q · 1/8 since 2018)

Recurring conceptual territories (a fact cluster asked in 4 or more papers)

  • MSP procurement · Agricultural economics (MSP, procurement, marketing, credit)7 papers · 2009–2023

7Question-generating concepts

The concepts that keep producing questions: what the examiner holds constant, and what changes between versions.

Crop and environment matching

Concept
Each crop has a climate, water, soil and season signature, and UPSC asks it in both directions.
Dimensions tested
description → crop; crop → climate/water need; crop → season; crop → region; crop → uses
How the examiner varied it
Description to crop (2010 pepper, 2020 cotton) → climate pair (2011 paddy and jute) → water efficiency (2021) → how many tea States (2022) → pea family (2024)
Facts that recur
Cotton needs 210 frost-free days and 50–100 cm of rain; sugarcane is the least water-efficient crop; groundnut, sesamum and pearl millet are mainly rainfed; groundnut, horse-gram and soybean are legumes
Adjacent knowledge
Major crops of India with season, climate, soil, leading States; New World and Old World crops

Resource-conserving practice → benefit

Concept
A farming practice is asked through the benefits it gives.
Dimensions tested
water saving; nutrient efficiency; emissions; soil health; weed and salinity control
How the examiner varied it
Climate-mitigating practice (2012 zero tillage) → water conservation (2017) → advantages multi-selects (2016 drip, 2020 fertigation, zero tillage, biochar, 2021 permaculture, 2022 SRI)
Facts that recur
Zero tillage allows wheat sowing without burning residue; neem coating slows urea dissolution; SRI saves seed and water; biochar improves water holding in growing media
Adjacent knowledge
Crop residue management, direct-seeded rice, laser levelling, natural farming, nano-urea

Price-support architecture

Concept
Who recommends, who approves, which crops, how procurement works.
Dimensions tested
CACP recommendation; CCEA approval; crop list; FRP vs SMP; procurement limits; FCI cost
How the examiner varied it
Statements (2009 number of crops, 2010 SMP) → approving body (2015 FRP) → crop list (2018) → procurement limits (2020) → niger MSP (2023)
Facts that recur
CCEA approves MSP and FRP; MSP covers barley, ragi, groundnut, sesamum among others, not areca or turmeric; procurement is not unlimited for all crops; FCI economic cost = MSP + procurement incidentals + distribution cost
Adjacent knowledge
PM-AASHA, buffer norms, open-market sale scheme, NBS fertiliser subsidy

GM technology

Concept
A GM crop is asked through its trait, gene source and the debate about it.
Dimensions tested
trait; gene source; crop; regulatory status; technique
How the examiner varied it
Trait of a named crop (2010 Mon 863, golden rice; 2011 Bt brinjal) → GM prospects and objections (2012) → GM mustard statements (2018) → Bollgard (2021)
Facts that recur
Golden rice has pro-vitamin A; Bt brinjal targets pests; GM mustard uses the barnase–barstar system; cytoplasmic male sterility and gene silencing are used to make transgenic crops
Adjacent knowledge
GEAC, Cartagena Protocol, gene editing (SDN-1/2), Bt cotton pink bollworm resistance

8Trap and distractor analysis

Fabricated claim12 · 15% of subject · 10% overall
Category swap8 · 10% of subject · 9% overall
Same-family mismatch8 · 10% of subject · 14% overall
Absolute qualifier7 · 9% of subject · 5% overall
Number / threshold6 · 7% of subject · 5% overall
Location swap4 · 5% of subject · 8% overall
Reversal / swapped roles4 · 5% of subject · 4% overall
Best-fit among true options3 · 4% of subject · 4% overall
Adjacent concept1 · 1% of subject · 3% overall
Scope / coverage1 · 1% of subject · 1% overall

32 of 82 questions (39%) had no identifiable trap: the right answer needed only the fact. Counts can exceed the number of questions because one question can carry two traps.

  • Invented benefits (FAB, 12): the multi-select adds one plausible-sounding benefit that the practice does not deliver.
  • Absolutes (ABS, 7): “procurement is unlimited for all cereals”, “the only means”, “market-driven price”.
  • Statistics (NUM, 6): land-use shares, the number of MSP crops, India vs China comparisons.

11Common note-worthiness

CORE

Asked in both exams; belongs in the shared base notes.

  • Crops: geography, climate, uses & statistics
  • Agricultural economics (MSP, procurement, marketing, credit)
  • Soil conservation & soil health

CORE + EXTENSION

Shared base, plus a layer that one exam goes deeper into.

  • Biotechnology & GM crops

EXAM-SPECIFIC

Mostly one exam’s territory; a separate layer.

  • Farming practices & technology

LOW-YIELD

Rare in both; a line or a table row, not a page.

  • Livestock & fisheries

12Frequency is not importance

  • Farming-practice questions (22, most of them practice → benefit) have no UPPCS counterpart, yet they are UPSC’s most stable agriculture template.
  • UPPCS asks agriculture as geography (9 Q) and as economy (9 Q); the common notes should keep the crop geography and the MSP/food-security parts shared.
  • Livestock has one question; keep it light.

13Cross-subject links

  • Economy: MSP, procurement, food security, subsidies, KCC and agri-marketing are also economic policy.
  • Environment: rice emissions, reactive nitrogen, biochar and conservation agriculture are climate topics.
  • Geography: soils, crop regions and irrigation.
  • Science & Technology: GM crops, gene silencing and genome sequencing.

14Repeated facts vs repeated relationships

1 linked pairs: conceptual 1. The later question is quoted; IDs are the compilation’s microtheme numbers.

TypeYearsWhat came back
Conceptual2010 → 2022Among the following crops, which one is the most important anthropogenic source of both methane and nitrous oxide?V1.32 · A1.4

Rice as a greenhouse-gas source (2010 → 2022) is the only linked pair; the stronger repetition is of form, since the “advantages of practice X” template recurs every few years.

  • In advantages multi-selects, check each listed benefit against the mechanism. An invented benefit is usually one that sounds like a different practice’s benefit.
  • In MSP questions, fix the institutional chain (CACP recommends, CCEA approves) before checking the crop list.

16Priority architecture

P1 · Build first

  • Crop requirements, seasons and regions
  • Resource-conserving practices and their benefits
  • MSP, procurement and marketing architecture

P2 · Build fully

  • GM crops and techniques
  • Soil conservation and soil health
  • Agricultural statistics (dated)

P3 · Build as tables

  • Agri schemes (grid)

P4 · Keep light

  • Livestock missions

17Subject summary

In numbers
82 questions (4.7% of the compilation) · static 70%, current-linked or dynamic 30% · recall 29%, concept 38%, application 5%, peripheral 28%
What the subject is
A practical, concept-heavy subject: UPSC asks what a farming practice achieves and how price support works.
Common core
Crop geography, MSP and procurement, soils.
UPSC extension
Practice-benefit questions, GM technology, fertiliser policy, statistics.
UPPCS extension
Agriculture as geography (producing States) and as economy (food security, land reform).
Dominant traps
Invented benefits and coverage absolutes.
How to build the notes
One crop table, one practice → benefit sheet, one price-support flowchart.

Environment & Ecology

290 of 1,759 questions · 16.5% of the compilation

Environment is split between direct questions (114, mostly “what is X” and “where is Y”) and multi-select (65), the subject’s favourite code format for listing organisms, pollutants or protected areas. Three-plus statement questions (55) are used for conventions and bodies. How-many formats (15, all but one in 2023–25) are used for species traits and native plants.

7Question-generating concepts

The concepts that keep producing questions: what the examiner holds constant, and what changes between versions.

Species profile

Concept
A species is asked through identity, class, habitat, distribution, behaviour and status.
Dimensions tested
is it found in India; where in India; which class; what behaviour; which threat; which status
How the examiner varied it
Where to see X (2016 hornbill, 2017 gharial, 2020 musk deer) → which occur naturally in India (2012, 2013, 2019) → classification (2020 birds, 2022 not a bird, 2024 insects) → how-many trait lists (2023 nocturnal, 2024 poisonous, parasitoids)
Facts that recur
Lion-tailed macaque in Tamil Nadu, Kerala and Karnataka; gharial on the Chambal; Kanha for the hard-ground barasingha; marsupials not native to India; ants cultivate fungi; honeybees waggle dance; fig wasps are the only pollinators of figs
Adjacent knowledge
Endemic species by region; IUCN categories for key Indian species; species recovery programmes

Initiative → launching body

Concept
An environmental initiative, fund or report is attributed to the body behind it and described by its purpose.
Dimensions tested
who launched it; intergovernmental or NGO; purpose; membership
How the examiner varied it
Direct (2015 BioCarbon Fund, 2018 Climate Neutral Now, 2023 invasive species group, 2025 ADB hub) → multi-statement on structure and purpose (2014 Earth Hour, 2015 IUCN/CITES and BirdLife, 2016 TEEB, 2017 GCCA and TRAFFIC, 2022 The Climate Group)
Facts that recur
Earth Hour is a WWF initiative; Climate Neutral Now is the UNFCCC secretariat’s; the BioCarbon Fund is the World Bank’s; TRAFFIC is a WWF–IUCN joint programme; the EU enacted the Nature Restoration Law
Adjacent knowledge
UNEP, UNDP, GEF, GCF, WWF, IUCN commissions; climate funds by source

Protected-area geography

Concept
A protected area is fixed by State, river, ecosystem type or a unique ecological trait.
Dimensions tested
State; river; hill range; climate zone; ecological role; legal category
How the examiner varied it
Descriptive location (2011 Bhitarkanika) → PA–river and PA–State pairs (2013, 2014) → unique trait (2015 floating Keibul Lamjao, climate range of Namdapha; 2017 EG–WG link; 2019 alpine) → basin multi-select (2020 Cauvery) → wetland–State how-many (2022)
Facts that recur
Keibul Lamjao has floating phumdis; Namdapha spans tropical to arctic climates; the Valley of Flowers is wholly alpine; Sathyamangalam links the Eastern and Western Ghats; Nagarjunsagar-Srisailam has the largest critical tiger habitat
Adjacent knowledge
A PA map by State with rivers; Ramsar sites by State; tiger reserves and elephant reserves

Law → body → implication

Concept
Each Indian environmental body sits under one Act, and each legal category has a specific consequence.
Dimensions tested
body → parent Act; schedule → implication; right-holder under FRA; nodal ministry; EPR under which rules
How the examiner varied it
Which Act (2014 AWBI, 2015 GEAC, 2022 CGWA) → schedule implication (2017 Schedule I, 2020 Schedule VI, 2024 vermin) → FRA authority and nodal ministry (2013, 2021)
Facts that recur
GEAC and CGWA are constituted under the EP Act 1986; Schedule I gives tiger-level protection; Schedule VI plants need a cultivation licence; the Gram Sabha initiates FRA claims; the Ministry of Tribal Affairs is the FRA nodal ministry
Adjacent knowledge
WPA 2022 amendment, Forest (Conservation) Amendment 2023, CAMPA Act, Biological Diversity Amendment 2023

Pollutant → source → effect

Concept
Each pollutant is tied to its sources and to its health or environmental effect.
Dimensions tested
source → pollutant; pollutant → effect; which gases make acid rain; which gases are in the AQI
How the examiner varied it
Acid rain (2010, 2013, 2022) → industry pollutant lists (2011, 2014, 2021) → emerging contaminants in products (2014 onwards: BPA, triclosan, microbeads, PFAS) → Statement-I/II on sources (2024, 2025)
Facts that recur
NOx and SO₂ cause acid rain; CO binds haemoglobin more strongly than oxygen; BPA is in polycarbonate; triclosan is in toiletries; fossil-fuel power plants are the largest SO₂ source
Adjacent knowledge
NAAQS, CAQM, PM2.5/PM10, persistent organic pollutants and the Stockholm list

Cycles and trophic logic

Concept
Matter and energy move through defined steps, and questions ask which process adds, removes or concentrates something.
Dimensions tested
cycle additions; trophic position; decomposers and detritivores; productivity order
How the examiner varied it
Biomagnification (2010) → carbon additions (2011 and 2014 identical) → nitrogen additions (2013) → productivity order (2013) → detritivores (2021) → phosphorus from weathering (2021)
Facts that recur
Respiration, decay and volcanic action add CO₂; photosynthesis removes it; the top predator accumulates most pesticide; earthworms, millipedes and woodlice are detritivores; mangroves are more productive than grasslands, lakes and oceans
Adjacent knowledge
Nitrogen fixers, ecological pyramids, succession stages

8Trap and distractor analysis

Category swap41 · 14% of subject · 9% overall
Same-family mismatch35 · 12% of subject · 14% overall
Fabricated claim32 · 11% of subject · 10% overall
Location swap26 · 9% of subject · 8% overall
Absolute qualifier15 · 5% of subject · 5% overall
Number / threshold12 · 4% of subject · 5% overall
Best-fit among true options11 · 4% of subject · 4% overall
Wrong cause–effect9 · 3% of subject · 2% overall
Adjacent concept5 · 2% of subject · 3% overall
Chronology slip5 · 2% of subject · 3% overall
Reversal / swapped roles5 · 2% of subject · 4% overall
Wrong legal source3 · 1% of subject · 1% overall
Scope / coverage1 · 0% of subject · 1% overall
Qualifier shift1 · 0% of subject · 1% overall

94 of 290 questions (32%) had no identifiable trap: the right answer needed only the fact. Counts can exceed the number of questions because one question can carry two traps.

  • Category swaps (CAT, 41) lead: an organism in the wrong class, a body under the wrong Act, a process in the wrong cycle.
  • Wrong parent body (MISM, 35): an initiative credited to a sister organisation (Earth Hour to UNEP and UNESCO rather than WWF).
  • Invented purposes (FAB, 32): an extra goal added to a fund or mission.
  • Location swaps (LOC, 26): a wetland, park or species put in the neighbouring State.

11Common note-worthiness

CORE

Asked in both exams; belongs in the shared base notes.

  • International conventions, bodies & initiatives
  • Pollution & pollutants
  • Protected areas, wetlands & reserves
  • Indian environmental laws & institutions
  • Ecology & ecosystem functioning
  • Climate change: science, mitigation & policy
  • Remediation & waste management
  • Biodiversity: patterns, hotspots, conservation

CORE + EXTENSION

Shared base, plus a layer that one exam goes deeper into.

  • Species: traits, habitats & distribution
  • Plants: uses, adaptations & tree species
  • Marine ecology

EXAM-SPECIFIC

Mostly one exam’s territory; a separate layer.

  • Bioenergy & bio-inputs

LOW-YIELD

Rare in both; a line or a table row, not a page.

None.

12Frequency is not importance

  • Species questions (47) outnumber every other Environment topic in UPSC but have only 8 UPPCS questions; they are a UPSC extension built on a small common base.
  • Initiatives and platforms (12 questions) are high-volume but low-transfer: each new platform is a new fact.
  • Ecology (22 UPSC, 33 UPPCS) is the shared conceptual base and the best value for the common notes.
  • Acid rain, carbon-cycle additions and desert leaf adaptations have each been asked more than once in almost the same words.

13Cross-subject links

  • Geography: biomes, wetlands, mangroves and protected-area locations.
  • Science & Technology: biotechnology for remediation, biofuels, GM crops, nanoparticles.
  • Agriculture: biofertilisers, reactive nitrogen, conservation agriculture, rice emissions.
  • Polity: Fifth Schedule, FRA and PESA in forest governance.
  • Economy: carbon markets, green finance, social cost of carbon.

14Repeated facts vs repeated relationships

3 linked pairs: exact 2, near 1. The later question is quoted; IDs are the compilation’s microtheme numbers.

TypeYearsWhat came back
Exact2011 → 2014Which of the following adds/add carbon dioxide to the carbon cycle on the planet Earth? 1. Volcanic action 2.…V6.11 · V1.21
Exact2013 → 2018Which of the following leaf modifications occur(s) in the desert areas to inhabit water loss? 1. Hard and waxy leaves…V3.13 · V3.8
Near2010 → 2013Acid rain is caused by the pollution of environment byV20.3 · V20.2

Three near-identical repeats: carbon-cycle additions (2011 → 2014), desert leaf adaptations (2013 → 2018) and acid-rain gases (2010 → 2013, asked again in 2022 as a four-item list).

15Elimination analysis

Statement, multi-select and pair questions (code options)Q%
Every item correct2819
No item correct43
Some items correct11378
Total145100
“How many” questions: keyed answerQ
Only one1
Only two5
Only three4
All4
None1
Total15
  • In “found naturally in India” lists, remove clearly exotic taxa first (spider monkey, marsupials, indri). This usually leaves one option.
  • For initiative questions, first decide whether the body is intergovernmental or an NGO; many distractors swap the two.
  • Carbon-cycle and acid-rain lists: one process or gas fixes the code (photosynthesis never adds CO₂; hydrogen oxides do not cause acid rain).

16Priority architecture

P1 · Build first

  • Ecology fundamentals (cycles, trophic, productivity)
  • Climate conventions and mechanisms
  • Pollutants and sources
  • Protected areas and wetlands
  • Environmental laws and bodies
  • Indian species profiles

P2 · Build fully

  • Climate science and carbon removal
  • Marine ecology
  • Plants: uses and native species
  • Biofuels and bio-inputs
  • Remediation and waste
  • Biodiversity patterns and hotspots

P3 · Build as tables

  • Initiatives and platforms (table)
  • Conservation organisations

P4 · Keep light

  • One-off indices and polls (Greendex)

17Subject summary

In numbers
290 questions (16.5% of the compilation) · static 70%, current-linked or dynamic 30% · recall 39%, concept 29%, application 3%, peripheral 29%
What the subject is
The second-largest subject; UPSC mixes ecology concepts with a long tail of species, bodies and initiatives.
Common core
Ecology, conventions, pollution, protected areas, environmental law, climate change.
UPSC extension
Species traits and distribution, initiatives and their sponsors, emerging contaminants, bioenergy.
UPPCS extension
Sustainable development and ecology at higher volume; observance days.
Dominant traps
Wrong class, wrong parent body, invented purpose.
How to build the notes
Keep concept notes for ecology and climate; keep species, PAs, bodies and initiatives as tables with one row per item.

197 of 1,759 questions · 11.2% of the compilation

Science is asked mostly through direct “what is X” questions (78) and three-plus statement sets (52) on a named technology. Multi-select (32) is used for applications (“in which of the following can X be used”). How-many formats (13) since 2023 carry the same application lists. There are almost no match, pair or sequence formats.

2018–25 window on the UPPCS scale: Easy 21, Moderate 71, Tough 2 of 94. Mean score 1.32 (all years).

Science & Technology has the highest concept-plus-application share of any subject (126 of 197, 64%) and the lowest peripheral share (6%). Questions are about what a technology does, and a clear understanding usually answers them; traps are relatively rare.

7Question-generating concepts

The concepts that keep producing questions: what the examiner holds constant, and what changes between versions.

Technology → principle → application

Concept
A named technology is asked through how it works and what it can or cannot do.
Dimensions tested
identify the term; underlying principle; applications; limits; comparison with a sibling technology
How the examiner varied it
What is X (2009 WiMAX, 2011 VPN, 2016 LiFi) → application multi-selects (2018 3D printing and GPS, 2019 wearables, 2020 AI and drones) → property statements (2019 AR/VR, 2022 Web 3.0, NFTs, SaaS) → how-many applications (2023 accelerometer, 2024 radar)
Facts that recur
LiFi and VLC use visible light; NFC is short-range and contactless; blockchain is a public distributed ledger; AI cannot yet fully replace judgement-based tasks; accelerometers trigger airbags, protect hard drives and rotate screens
Adjacent knowledge
Semiconductors and chips, 5G/6G, edge computing, digital public infrastructure

Biotechnology mechanism

Concept
A biotech tool is asked through what it does to DNA, cells or the immune system, and what it enables.
Dimensions tested
tool → mechanism; tool → application; ethical or regulatory issue
How the examiner varied it
What is X (2016 transcriptome, 2019 Cas9, 2020 pronuclear transfer) → applications (2017 genome sequencing in agriculture, 2019 RNAi, 2022 DNA barcoding) → vaccine platforms (2020 PCV, 2021 recombinant vector, 2022 COVID vaccines) → monoclonal antibodies (2025)
Facts that recur
Cas9 is a molecular scissor; RNAi silences genes; pronuclear transfer prevents mitochondrial disease; DNA barcoding identifies species; recombinant vector vaccines use a harmless virus carrying the antigen gene
Adjacent knowledge
mRNA vaccines, gene therapy, CAR-T, biosimilars, GEAC and biosafety rules

Pathogen and disease

Concept
A disease is asked through pathogen type, transmission, vector, vaccine and eradication status.
Dimensions tested
pathogen class; transmission route; vector; vaccine availability; eradication
How the examiner varied it
Hepatitis B vs HIV (2010, 2019) → transmission by tattooing (2013) → outbreak geography (2015 Ebola) → vectors (2017 Zika, 2023 Wolbachia) → receptors and resistance (2019 AMR, 2021 ACE2)
Facts that recur
Hepatitis B spreads like HIV and has a vaccine, Hepatitis C does not; Zika shares the dengue vector; smallpox is eradicated; Wolbachia is used to curb mosquito-borne viruses
Adjacent knowledge
TB, malaria and dengue programmes; zoonoses; the One Health approach

Everyday science reasoning

Concept
A familiar phenomenon explained by a physical or chemical principle.
Dimensions tested
why X happens; what X depends on
How the examiner varied it
Why questions, mostly 2009–2014 (frozen lakes, satellites, radiators, capillarity, rainbows, chlorination, cold storage) → occasional return (2021 pressure cooker, water as a solvent)
Facts that recur
Water is densest at 4 °C, so lakes freeze from the top; a pressure cooker’s temperature depends on the vent area and lid weight; chlorine disinfects through hypochlorous acid
Adjacent knowledge
Heat transfer, surface tension, pressure–boiling point, refraction and dispersion

Space systems and astronomy

Concept
Satellites and missions by function; stars and waves by physics.
Dimensions tested
orbit type; navigation vs remote sensing; mission objective; stellar end state; detector purpose
How the examiner varied it
Mission identification (2009, 2016) → system statements (2018 PSLV/GSLV and IRNSS, 2025 GAGAN) → gravitational waves (2017 eLISA, 2019 black-hole merger, 2020 LISA triangle) → stellar objects (2023, 2024)
Facts that recur
PSLV launches Earth-observation satellites to sun-synchronous orbit and GSLV takes communication satellites to GTO; IRNSS has satellites in GSO and GEO; LISA is a triangle of three spacecraft; stars below 1.44 solar masses end as white dwarfs
Adjacent knowledge
Chandrayaan and Aditya-L1, NavIC coverage, Gaganyaan, LIGO-India

Energy transition technologies

Concept
Low-carbon technologies asked through what they emit, store or need.
Dimensions tested
emission; efficiency; material inputs; industrial use; storage role
How the examiner varied it
Fuel-cell exhaust (2010, 2024 near-identical) → fuel-cell and H-CNG statements (2015, 2019) → green hydrogen uses (2023 ×2) → storage and DERs (2024) → battery cathodes and powertrains (2025)
Facts that recur
A hydrogen fuel-cell vehicle emits water; green hydrogen can decarbonise fertiliser, refining and steel; cobalt, lithium and nickel make cathodes and graphite makes anodes; pumped storage is energy storage
Adjacent knowledge
Electrolyser types, solid-state batteries, sodium-ion, small modular reactors

8Trap and distractor analysis

Reversal / swapped roles11 · 6% of subject · 4% overall
Same-family mismatch8 · 4% of subject · 14% overall
Best-fit among true options8 · 4% of subject · 4% overall
Absolute qualifier6 · 3% of subject · 5% overall
Number / threshold5 · 3% of subject · 5% overall
Fabricated claim5 · 3% of subject · 10% overall
Location swap2 · 1% of subject · 8% overall
Chronology slip2 · 1% of subject · 3% overall
Category swap1 · 1% of subject · 9% overall

150 of 197 questions (76%) had no identifiable trap: the right answer needed only the fact. Counts can exceed the number of questions because one question can carry two traps.

  • Reversals (REV, 11) are the main trap: two paired technologies or organisms with properties swapped (AR vs VR, single- vs double-stranded genomes, PSLV vs GSLV roles, giant vs dwarf star lifespans).
  • Absolutes (ABS, 6): “no virus can …”, “nanoparticles do not exist in nature”, “all UAVs can land vertically”.
  • Most Science questions carry no trap at all: the key is understanding the technology.

11Common note-worthiness

CORE

Asked in both exams; belongs in the shared base notes.

  • Health, diseases & nutrition
  • Physics & everyday science
  • Biology: cells, microbes, plants, evolution
  • Defence technology
  • Chemistry & materials in daily life

CORE + EXTENSION

Shared base, plus a layer that one exam goes deeper into.

  • Energy technologies
  • Computing & digital technologies
  • Genetics & biotechnology
  • Astronomy & astrophysics
  • Space technology & applications
  • Communication technologies

EXAM-SPECIFIC

Mostly one exam’s territory; a separate layer.

  • Nanotechnology & advanced materials

LOW-YIELD

Rare in both; a line or a table row, not a page.

None.

12Frequency is not importance

  • UPPCS General Science is chemistry- and body-heavy (74 of 133 questions); UPSC puts more weight on applied technology. The common base is health, biology and basic physics.
  • Computing and space technology (34 UPSC questions) have only 3 UPPCS questions as static science, but UPPCS asks S&T in the news (14 Q) under Current Affairs.
  • Everyday-science why questions were frequent before 2015 and rare since; they are still the cheapest to prepare.

13Cross-subject links

  • Environment: biofuels, remediation, green hydrogen, nanoparticles as pollutants.
  • Agriculture: GM crops, genome sequencing, biofertilisers.
  • Economy: digital payments, blockchain and CBDC.
  • Geography: EM spectrum and the atmosphere, energy resources.
  • Current affairs: defence induction and missions.

16Priority architecture

P1 · Build first

  • Health: pathogens, vaccines, nutrition
  • Biotechnology tools and applications
  • Digital technologies (blockchain, AI, quantum, cyber)
  • Space systems and navigation
  • Energy technologies (hydrogen, storage, batteries, nuclear)

17Subject summary

In numbers
197 questions (11.2% of the compilation) · static 42%, current-linked or dynamic 58% · recall 30%, concept 50%, application 14%, peripheral 6%
What the subject is
The most current-linked static subject (52% current-linked): UPSC picks technologies in the news and asks the underlying science.
Common core
Health and disease, biology, basic physics and chemistry.
UPSC extension
Applied technology: digital, biotech, space, energy transition, defence.
UPPCS extension
Chemistry and human-body facts at higher volume; scientists and inventions.
Dominant traps
Swapped properties of paired technologies; few traps overall.
How to build the notes
One page per technology family: principle → capability → limits → Indian programme; keep the basic-science layer shared with UPPCS.

International Relations uses direct questions (36) for “in the context of which country/grouping” items, statements (29) for groupings and treaties, and multi-select (10) for memberships. 7Question-generating concepts

The concepts that keep producing questions: what the examiner holds constant, and what changes between versions.

Membership

Concept
Who belongs to which grouping, and who does not.
Dimensions tested
founding members; current members; non-members; India’s membership; observer status
How the examiner varied it
Not a member (2009 G7, 2016 GCC) → multi-select members (2009 EU and ASEAN, 2014 Arctic Council, 2022 Turkic States) → India’s memberships (2015, 2022) → how many are NATO members (2025)
Facts that recur
Iran is not in the GCC; India is in the EAS, SCO, AIIB and MTCR but not APEC or ASEAN; Denmark, Russia and the USA are Arctic Council members; Azerbaijan and Uzbekistan are OTS members
Adjacent knowledge
A membership matrix for 20 groupings with India’s status; recent expansions (BRICS, NATO)

Place → country → reason

Concept
A place in the news is tested by locating it and saying why it is in the news.
Dimensions tested
region → country; country → cause of news; conflict → parties; camp → host country
How the examiner varied it
Direct (2009 Elephant Pass, Dadaab) → pair lists (2014, 2016, 2018 ×2) → how-many pairs (2022 regions and countries, 2023 conflict areas, 2024 reasons in news) → Statement-I/II on the Sahel (2024)
Facts that recur
Chechnya is in Russia; Mindanao in the Philippines; Cabo Delgado in Mozambique; Mazar-i-Sharif in Afghanistan; Donbas in Ukraine; Tigray in Ethiopia; Kachin in Myanmar
Adjacent knowledge
A running world map with conflict zones, chokepoints and camps (links to Geography location)

Treaty and regime content

Concept
What a treaty or regime requires, who is in it, and what membership brings.
Dimensions tested
parties; obligations; India’s status; consequences of joining
How the examiner varied it
What is X (2011 New START) → nuclear-weapon states under the NPT (2015) → consequences of NSG membership and the Additional Protocol (2018) → why some reactors are safeguarded (2020) → UNCLOS rights (2022)
Facts that recur
Only the five NPT nuclear-weapon states (incl. China and France) are recognised; the Additional Protocol brings civilian reactors under IAEA safeguards; imported-fuel reactors are safeguarded; Nuclear Security Summits were not UN-convened
Adjacent knowledge
CTBT, TPNW, MTCR, Wassenaar, Australia Group, OPCW, BWC, Hague Code

UN structure

Concept
Composition, terms and procedures of UN organs and agencies.
Dimensions tested
size; term; who appoints; what the organ can do
How the examiner varied it
Numbers (2009 ECOSOC, UNSC) → committee and observer procedures (2022) → agencies and years (2017, 2021, 2025)
Facts that recur
ECOSOC has 54 members; non-permanent UNSC members serve two-year terms; the Credentials Committee is appointed by the UNGA; the UNGA can grant observer status to non-members
Adjacent knowledge
UN reform, peacekeeping, specialised agencies and headquarters

Number / threshold5 · 5% of subject · 5% overall
Chronology slip4 · 4% of subject · 3% overall
Absolute qualifier4 · 4% of subject · 5% overall
Wrong cause–effect2 · 2% of subject · 2% overall
Reversal / swapped roles2 · 2% of subject · 4% overall

22 of 96 questions (23%) had no identifiable trap: the right answer needed only the fact. Counts can exceed the number of questions because one question can carry two traps.

  • Wrong grouping or country (MISM 18, LOC 14): a real region paired with the wrong country (Darfur with Mali, Crimea with Hungary, Cabo Delgado with Spain).
  • Category swaps (CAT, 15): a non-member presented as a member, an NGO as a UN body.
  • Invented reasons (FAB, 11): plausible but wrong “reason for being in the news”.

12Frequency is not importance

  • IR looks small in UPSC (96) but UPPCS asks international relations in almost every paper (46 Q in 8 years); the membership matrix and map serve both.
  • Nuclear regimes (8 UPSC questions) are UPSC-specific but conceptually stable: the same regimes return with different angles.
  • Individual NGOs and summits are low-yield.

13Cross-subject links

  • Economy: WTO, IMF, World Bank, AIIB and NDB are analysed under Economy (international economic institutions).
  • Geography: places in the news are location questions; chokepoints and seas.
  • Environment: climate conventions and environmental bodies are analysed under Environment.
  • Polity: treaty implementation (Article 253).

14Repeated facts vs repeated relationships

No question-to-question repeats were found in this subject.

No question repeats directly, but the region → country pair list recurs as a format (2014, 2016, 2018 ×2, 2022 ×2, 2023 ×2, 2024).

17Subject summary

In numbers
96 questions (5.5% of the compilation) · static 35%, current-linked or dynamic 65% · recall 61%, concept 5%, application 1%, peripheral 32%
What the subject is
A current-affairs subject with static anchors: membership rules, maps and treaty content.
Common core
Groupings and memberships, places in the news, UN structure.
UPSC extension
Nuclear regimes, EU institutions, how-many region lists.
UPPCS extension
Heads of government, summit hosts and logos, neighbourhood projects.
Dominant traps
Right place, wrong country; non-member as member.
How to build the notes
Maintain two living tables (membership matrix, places map) and one static sheet for treaties and the UN.

7Question-generating concepts

The concepts that keep producing questions: what the examiner holds constant, and what changes between versions.

Single-event statement set

Concept
One major event of the preceding year is asked through three or four factual statements.
Dimensions tested
venue; winners; first-time facts; format rules
How the examiner varied it
Match lists of people (2009) → statement sets on one event (2021 Olympics, WTC, Laureus; 2023 Chess Olympiad; 2025 Kho Kho World Cup, Chess Olympiad)
Facts that recur
The 44th Chess Olympiad (2022) was the first held in India; the first Kho Kho World Cup was held in Delhi in 2025
Adjacent knowledge
Major sporting events of the year and India’s results

Award rules

Concept
National awards asked through their field, eligibility, jury and legal status.
Dimensions tested
field; jury composition; maximum number; constitutional status
How the examiner varied it
Field (2009 Bhatnagar) → rules and status (2021 Bharat Ratna/Padma) → sports-award criteria (2023) → jury (2025 Gandhi Peace Prize)
Facts that recur
Bharat Ratna and Padma awards are not titles under Article 18(1); at most three Bharat Ratnas a year; the Gandhi Peace Prize jury includes the PM, the CJI and the Leader of Opposition
Adjacent knowledge
Gallantry awards, Sahitya/Sangeet Natak awards, Jnanpith

8Trap and distractor analysis

Same-family mismatch14 · 33% of subject · 14% overall
Fabricated claim5 · 12% of subject · 10% overall
Category swap3 · 7% of subject · 9% overall
Wrong legal source2 · 5% of subject · 1% overall
Location swap2 · 5% of subject · 8% overall
Adjacent concept1 · 2% of subject · 3% overall
Number / threshold1 · 2% of subject · 5% overall
Wrong cause–effect1 · 2% of subject · 2% overall

14 of 43 questions (33%) had no identifiable trap: the right answer needed only the fact. Counts can exceed the number of questions because one question can carry two traps.

  • Same-family mismatch (MISM, 14): real people, companies or awards paired wrongly.
  • Invented event facts (FAB, 5): wrong mottos, first winners or scores.

11Common note-worthiness

CORE

Asked in both exams; belongs in the shared base notes.

None.

CORE + EXTENSION

Shared base, plus a layer that one exam goes deeper into.

  • Awards & honours
  • Defence institutions & exercises

EXAM-SPECIFIC

Mostly one exam’s territory; a separate layer.

  • Sports
  • Persons, books & authors

LOW-YIELD

Rare in both; a line or a table row, not a page.

  • Companies & places

12Frequency is not importance

  • UPSC’s pure current affairs has almost disappeared outside single-event statement sets, while UPPCS devotes about 17% of its paper to Current Affairs & Miscellaneous (199 of 1,200, including UP affairs).
  • Award rules (not recipients) are the only part with static value for both exams.

13Cross-subject links

  • Polity: Article 18 and awards; defence institutions and the Union executive.
  • Art & culture: artists and cultural institutions.
  • Science & Technology: defence technology.

14Repeated facts vs repeated relationships

No question-to-question repeats were found in this subject.

16Priority architecture

    • Award rules

    17Subject summary

    In numbers
    43 questions (2.4% of the compilation) · static 44%, current-linked or dynamic 56% · recall 42%, concept 2%, application 0%, peripheral 56%
    What the subject is
    A residual layer: pure current affairs, heavy in 2009, now a few statement sets on single events and institutions.
    Common core
    Award rules and defence organisation.
    UPSC extension
    Event-level statement sets.
    UPPCS extension
    UP affairs, sports, awards and books at much higher volume, sociology.
    Dominant traps
    Wrong pairings and invented event facts.
    How to build the notes
    Keep this out of the static notes; maintain a year-wise current-affairs file with an awards-and-events page.

    Common notes architecture

    Subject → major topic → sub-topic → core concept → fact clusters → layers

    The architecture below turns the evidence into a build order for one set of notes serving both exams. Each subject lists its major topics in priority order. Under each topic: the core concept that belongs in the shared notes, the recurring fact clusters (sub-topics) with their question counts, and the three layers that sit on top of the shared base: UPSC-only, UPPCS-only and current affairs.

    PriorityTopicsUPSC Q%Cumulative %
    P148107361.061.0
    P25156131.992.9
    P3141086.199.0
    P45171.0100.0

    P1 = build first, in full; P2 = build fully after P1; P3 = build as tables; P4 = keep to a line or a row. Priority weighs frequency, spread across papers, conceptual reach and value for both exams, so it does not follow question counts alone.

    Appendix: answer-key corrections

    30 questions where the compilation’s key was corrected

    YearQuestionKeyWhy
    2009G1.19Consider the following statements : 1. Infant mortality rate takes into account the death of infants within a…(b) → (d)IMR counts deaths within the first year, per 1,000 live births: neither statement is correct.
    2009P3.19Consider the following statements : 1. The Advocate General of a State in India is appointed by the President…(b) → (d)The Governor appoints the Advocate General, and High Courts have no advisory jurisdiction: neither statement is correct.
    2010E3.23Inclusive growth as enunciated in the 11th Five Year Plan does not include on of the following:(c) → (d)Word-for-word the same as the 2009 question, which the compilation keys correctly (strengthening of capital market); the 2010 key is a slip.
    2010G6.15A geographic area with an altitude of 400 metres has following characteristics shown in table below. If this…(a) → (d)Uniform high temperatures and rain in every month point to a tropical rainforest.
    2011V3.15When the bark of a tree is removed in a circular fashion all around near its base, it gradually dries up and…(a) → (b)Girdling cuts the phloem, so the roots are starved of food (energy).
    2014S7.8Consider the following pairs : Vitamin : Deficiency disease 1. Vitamin C : Scurvy 2. Vitamin D : Rickets 3.…(d) → (a)Vitamin C–scurvy and vitamin D–rickets are right; night blindness is vitamin A.
    2014V1.23There is some concern regarding the nanoparticles of some chemical elements that are used by the industry in…(b) → (d)All three concerns about industrial nanoparticles are recognised.
    2016G2.13Recently, linking of which of the following rivers was undertaken?(d) → (b)The Godavari–Krishna link (Polavaram–Pattiseema, 2015–16) is the linking referred to.
    2016N2.17Project Loon’, sometimes seen in the news, is related to(a) → (b)Project Loon was a balloon-based wireless internet project.
    2016V2.15What is/are the importance/ importances of the ‘United Convention to Combat Desertification’? 1. It aims to…(a) → (c)The UNCCD has no special focus on South Asia and North Africa; statements 1 and 3 are right.
    2017C25.3Which one of the following was a very important seaport in the Kakatiya kingdom ?(c) → (b)Motupalli was the Kakatiya port described by Marco Polo.
    2017E7.5Consider the following statements : 1. Tax revenue as a percent of GDP of India has steadily increased in the…(a) → (d)Neither India’s tax-to-GDP ratio nor its fiscal-deficit ratio rose steadily over 2007–2017.
    2017M10.4The Trade Disputes Act of 1929 provided for(a) → (d)The 1929 Act set up courts of inquiry and boards of conciliation and restricted strikes: option (d).
    2018A2.13With reference to the circumstances in Indian agriculture, the concept of “Conservation Agriculture” assumes…(d) → (c)Conservation agriculture rests on minimum tillage, residue cover and crop rotation (2, 4 and 5).
    2018A3.6Consider the following: 1. Areca nut 2. Barley 3. Coffee 4. Finger millet 5. Groundnut 6. Sesamum 7. Turmeric…(c) → (b)Barley, ragi, groundnut and sesamum have MSPs; areca nut, coffee and turmeric do not.
    2018G17.1Consider the following statements: 1.The Barren Island volcano is an active volcano located in the Indian…(d) → (a)Barren Island is active, but it is not 140 km east of Great Nicobar and it erupted after 1991: only statement 1 is correct.
    2018G3.12Consider the following pairs : Regions sometimes mentioned in news Country 1. Catalonia - Spain 2. Crimea -…(b) → (c)Crimea is not in Hungary and Oromia is not in Nigeria; Catalonia–Spain and Mindanao–Philippines are right.
    2018M3.4With reference to educational institutes during colonial rule in India, consider the following pairs :…(c) → (b)Sanskrit College, Benares was founded by Jonathan Duncan and Fort William College by Richard (not Arthur) Wellesley; only the Calcutta Madrasa pair is right.
    2018S4.14Consider the following pairs : Terms sometimes seen in news Context /Topic Belle II experiment Artificial…(a) → (b)Belle II is a particle-physics experiment and CRISPR–Cas9 is gene editing; only the blockchain pair is right.
    2018V17.1Which of the following statements best describes “carbon fertilization”?(b) → (a)Carbon fertilisation is increased plant growth under higher CO₂.
    2018V5.6In which one of the following States is Pakhui Wildlife Sanctuary located?(b) → (a)Pakhui (Pakke) Wildlife Sanctuary is in Arunachal Pradesh.
    2018V7.4With reference to the ‘Global Alliance for Climate-Smart Agriculture (GACSA)’, which of the following…(c) → (b)GACSA was launched at the 2014 UN Climate Summit and India did not create it; only statement 2 holds.
    2019V16.5Recently, there was a growing awareness in our country about the importance of Himalayan nettle (Girardinia…(a) → (d)The Himalayan nettle drew attention as a source of textile fibre.
    2022C18.1With reference to Indian history, consider the following pairs : Historical person Known as 1. Aryadeva —…(b) → (c)Aryadeva was a Buddhist (Madhyamaka) scholar; the other two pairs are right, so two pairs are correct.
    2023M2.3Consider the following statements in respect of the Constitution Day: Statement-I: The Constitution Day is…(a) → (c)The Drafting Committee was set up in August 1947, not on 26 November 1949: Statement II is wrong.
    2023P2.4Consider the following statements: 1. According to the Constitution of India, the Central Government has a…(a) → (b)Articles 355 and 22(3) make statements 1 and 2 correct; POTA allowed confessions to police, so only two statements are correct.
    2025E7.1Consider the following statements: Statement I: In India, income from allied agricultural activities like…(b) → (d)Poultry and wool rearing are not agricultural income under the Income-tax Act, so Statement I is wrong while Statement II is right.
    2025I2.1India is one of the founding members of the International North-South Transport Corridor (INSTC), a…(d) → (a)The INSTC links India to Central Asia and Europe via Iran.
    2025P11.2Consider the following statements about Lokpal: I. The power of Lokpal applies to public servants of India,…(b) → (a)Only statement III (minimum age 45) holds under the Lokpal Act; it covers public servants abroad, does not require a CJI as Chairperson, and can inquire into a Prime Minister with safeguards.
    2025S8.1Consider the following statements: I. Indian Railways have prepared a National Rail Plan (NRP) to create a…(d) → (a)The National Rail Plan targets 2030 and Kavach was developed indigenously by RDSO; statements I and II are not correct.

    Also excluded from all counts: S10.3, a second printing of the 2018 PSLV/GSLV question (S10.2). One 2017 Economy question (E10.9) is printed without its statement list in the source; it is counted and tagged from its stem and options.

    Source: Civilsdaily, “Microtheme Prelims [PYQs 2009–2025]” (1,760 entries, 1,759 unique questions). UPPCS figures: the UPPCS Prelims PYQ Analysis, 2018–2025 (1,200 questions). All counts computed from question-level tags; every subject and year total reconciles to 1,759.
